# ISBI paper progress (as of 2026-10-27)

Working state of the 4-page ISBI submission (deadline 2026-11-02, 23:59 US Eastern; moved from 2026-10-26, checked on biomedicalimaging.org/2027 on 2026-10-25; plan: `docs/roadmap.md`,
CGM as built: `docs/experiment7.md`). This file only holds what the paper needs: scope,
claims with their evidence, runs in flight and the paper tables.
- `docs/experiment-log.md`: every roadmap experiment (status table, decisions D0–D4 with
  evidence, readings, open points outside the paper, the other generated tables).
- `docs/findings.md`: lessons that outlive single runs (protocol, benchmark quirks,
  negative results).
- Setup: `docs/benchmarks.md` (PLISM/HEST), `docs/parity-audit.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (contract,
  caches, commands). CATS phase: `docs/archive/progress-cats-scanner-removal.md`.

Tables (section 4) are generated by `uv run python -m sipe.eval.results_tables --write`;
numbers in the prose are copied from them, with the run dir.


Host scripts named below (`scripts/*.sh`, gitignored) were removed on 2026-10-20, except
`scripts/cgm_arms_fits.sh`; every run dir keeps what was run (`config.yaml`, `git.txt`,
bench `provenance.json`).

## 1. Scope and story (decided 2026-10-15)

**G0 → CGM at fixed tile-level pairing** (consensus over the 5 scanner views of a SCORPION
tile): with tile-level paired data, does a consensus generative model trained through the
frozen backbone (CGM, Exp 7) improve over its linear limit G0 on PLISM, while keeping HEST?
Inference is one global map on one H0-mini GAP embedding: no scanner ID, no paired view.

- Rows in the paper: raw H0-mini GAP; the published eraser (slide-level pairing, λ=0.5) as the
  external baseline; G0 (closed-form consensus shrinkage, CGM's linear limit); the eraser at
  tile level (same pairing unit, objective control); CGM V0-S (linear encoder, ablation); CGM
  V1-S (selected arm, three seeds 7b71 / 8ed0 / ac94). Ablations in the text or supplement: V2-S, no
  augmentation, untrained init, LOSO.
- Framing: most of the PLISM gain over G0 is already in the *trained linear* arm V0-S; the
  nonlinear encoder adds a small, consistent amount. The contribution is the generative
  training (per-scanner likelihood, ELBO through the backbone), the nonlinearity is an
  ablation. The per-scanner likelihood is a small part: the exchangeable linear V0 is
  −0.003 below V0-S and +0.022 over G0 (claim 6), so most of the gain is training an
  exchangeable model through the backbone. The exchangeable nonlinear V1 gives the same
  picture for the selected arm: −0.001 below V1-S, +0.027 over G0 (claim 6).
- Decisions behind the scope (evidence in `docs/experiment-log.md`, section 1): D0 eraser =
  bar to beat (2026-10-13); D3 no Gaussian mixtures (2026-10-14); D2 pairing fixed at tile
  level (2026-10-15); D4 no quadratic correction (2026-10-15). D5/D6 not taken.
- Out of scope, re-evaluated in the follow-up paper in a broader setup: pairing scale
  (region / tile erasers; the region eraser is the strongest linear map on PLISM), quadratic
  correction, Gaussian mixtures, PairedVAE, Exp 5/6.
- Report order: SCORPION val (selection) → SCORPION test → PLISM → HEST. PLISM: full tables as
  the main result, the without-HR tables alongside with the reason (`docs/findings.md`, HR).
- PLISM metric in the paper (decision 2026-10-21, user): mean top-1 over slide pairs, as in
  the claims and the per-pair intervals (blocks `plism`, `plism_pairs`). Median top-1 is at
  the floor for weak backbones (UNI2-h raw, all pairs: median 0.005 vs mean 0.043). The
  plismbench leaderboard metrics (medians of cosine (all) and top-10 per shift group, plus
  their mean; blocks `plism_leaderboard`, which reproduce the README rows of H0-Mini and
  UNI2-h exactly) are kept for comparability with the leaderboard; no paired intervals on
  them. Official H0-Mini (0.541) is the CLS token; our raw row is GAP (0.709).

| 1 | CGM beats its linear limit G0 on PLISM | V1-S 7b71 vs G0, all pairs +0.028 [+0.024, +0.032], better on 98% of pairs; seed 8ed0 0.322 vs 7b71 0.321 on all pairs; seed 44 (`runs/bench/2026-10-16_22-18-40_lxbelshark_bec7_cgm_v1s_seed44_ac94`) +0.028 [+0.023, +0.032], 98% (per-pair vs G0; PLISM) | done, 3 seeds |
| 2 | the gain is largest under the joint scanner + staining shift | 7b71 vs G0: scanner + staining +0.031 [+0.026, +0.035], inter-staining +0.018 [+0.015, +0.022], inter-scanner +0.012 [+0.007, +0.020]; HR inter-scanner +0.001 [−0.007, +0.012] (21 pairs) | done |
| 3 | it keeps downstream utility (HEST) | average 0.363 for 7b71 (`runs/bench/2026-10-15_07-47-25_lxbelshark_8823_cgm_v1s_7b71`), V0-S 0.362, G0 0.363, GAP 0.364, tile eraser 0.363 (`runs/bench/2026-10-17_11-26-14_lxbelshark_65e7_softshrink_gap_tile_lam0.4`); per task CGM moves more than G0 (HEST table). | 13 | (analysis on H0-mini, nothing selected; likely in the paper, user 2026-10-25) CGM's lead over G0 does not depend on the number of training scanners | Scanner-count tables (section 4): maps fitted on n = 2, 3, 4 of AT2 / DP200 / GT450 / Philips (every subset; P1000 excluded for its registration offset) and on all five; fixed image budget of 512 per step, so fewer scanners does not mean less data. SCORPION test (`runs/bench/2026-10-24_18-06-13_lxbelshark_29f2_scorpion_scanner_count_test`): V1-S − G0 of the same subset +0.0227 [+0.0187, +0.0268] at n = 2, +0.0164 at n = 3, +0.0126 at n = 4, +0.0124 with all five; at n = 2 +0.0249 on pairs with an unseen scanner vs +0.0119 on seen pairs. V0-S − G0 +0.0197 [+0.0157, +0.0238] at n = 2, +0.0052 with all five. PLISM all pairs: G0 0.262 / 0.279 / 0.289 / 0.294 for n = 2 / 3 / 4 / 5, V1-S 0.291 / 0.310 / 0.320 / 0.321, V0-S 0.290 / 0.307 / 0.316 / 0.318; V1-S − G0 +0.029 [+0.024, +0.033] at n = 2, +0.028 with all five (99% / 98% of pairs); V0-S − G0 +0.028 [+0.023, +0.032] at n = 2, +0.025 with all five. Inter-scanner: V1-S − G0 +0.022 [+0.015, +0.032] at n = 2 vs +0.012 with all five. Both trained maps beat the G0 of their own subset in all 10 subsets (per-subset PLISM block in `docs/experiment-log.md`). PLISM pairs whose two scanners were not in the fit subset (AT2, GT450, P = Philips matched by model; n = 2): V1-S +0.022 [+0.015, +0.032], V0-S +0.022 [+0.014, +0.031]. PLISM for the sc_* maps through `sipe.bench.plism_apply` on stored raw features; check `runs/bench/2026-10-23_15-00-18_lxbelshark_f206_scapply_check_g0_full` = `mixture_G0_K1` per pair (max abs top-1 difference 2.5e-4) | read 2026-10-24 (V1-S) and 2026-10-25 (V0-S): every model gains from more scanners, the trained maps do not need more of them than G0; on PLISM the linear V0-S has almost all of the lead at every n (claim 6), on SCORPION the nonlinear share is smaller at n = 2 than with all five. One fit per subset, n = 4 a single subset; the seen / unseen PLISM groups differ in pair difficulty, so only "positive on unseen scanners" is solid |
| 14 | (pooling sensitivity: CONCH v1.5 on patch-token GAP, one seed, nothing re-selected) CGM's gain over G0 on CONCH v1.5 does not depend on the official head | CONCH v1.5 GAP tables (section 4; run dirs in `runs/conch15_gap_manifest.env`; fit and G0 at 98d5bdf, the rest at 816db3e (docs-only change), all clean): raw GAP is more robust than the raw head, all pairs 0.095 vs 0.047, +0.048 [+0.042, +0.056], better on 93% of pairs (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_00-43-42_lxbelshark_0e13_conch15_gap_ref`), but far below H0-mini GAP (0.227, claim 4) and UNI2-h GAP (0.333, claim 11). G0 0.164 (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_03-13-22_lxbelshark_26a4_conch15_gap_g0`), V1-S 1ee1 0.206 (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_00-43-42_lxbelshark_6c45_cgm_v1s_conch15_gap_1ee1`). V1-S vs G0, all pairs +0.041 [+0.036, +0.047], better on 99% of pairs; inter-scanner +0.058 [+0.048, +0.068] (100%), inter-staining +0.031 [+0.026, +0.036], joint +0.042 [+0.035, +0.048]; without HR all pairs G0 0.190, V1-S 0.238. V1-S on GAP vs V1-S on the head, all pairs +0.103 [+0.090, +0.117]. Leaderboard metric (medians): raw 0.519, G0 0.644, V1-S 0.699. SCORPION (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_00-06-32_lxbelshark_cea6_scorpion_conch15_gap_cgm_select`): val top-1 raw 0.3685, G0 0.9558, V1-S 0.9792 (+0.0234 over G0); test 0.3008 / 0.9172 / 0.9567. In-loop training (`runs/2026-10-25_18-34-14_lxbelshark_1ee1`, 5999/6000): nonlinear_rel 0.070 (head 0.120). HEST average: raw 0.364 (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_05-46-52_lxbelshark_92c3_conch15_gap_ref`), G0 0.362 (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_07-13-24_lxbelshark_f668_conch15_gap_g0`), V1-S 0.358 (`runs/bench/2026-10-26_05-46-52_lxbelshark_039c_cgm_v1s_conch15_gap_1ee1`); paired G0 vs raw −0.002 [−0.006, +0.002]; V1-S vs raw −0.007 [−0.014, +0.000] (9/29 folds better), vs G0 −0.004 [−0.009, −0.000]; per task vs raw, gene-bootstrap intervals exclude 0 for READ −0.025, SKCM −0.025, LYMPH_IDC −0.008, IDC −0.006 (down). Raw GAP vs raw head on HEST +0.016 [+0.006, +0.029] (22/29 folds) | read 2026-10-27: the gain over G0 transfers and is larger than on the head (+0.041 vs +0.019 all pairs), positive in every shift type; as for UNI2-h, the pooling sets the raw level (GAP above head on PLISM and on HEST). HEST: G0 free, V1-S a small cost like UNI2-h GAP (vs raw the interval touches 0, vs G0 it excludes 0 barely). One seed, H0-mini hyperparameters |

Caveats to state in the paper: one SCORPION split (5 val and 5 test WSIs), SCORPION val is
near ceiling (top-1 differences of a few thousandths); hyperparameters not tuned; V2-S, the
no-aug fits, the UNI2-h fits, the CONCH v1.5 fits (head, GAP) and the scanner-count fits (one per subset) have one seed; LOSO on SCORPION is not unseen-scanner generalization in
general.

ablation: the closed-form init with stronger attenuation (β=3.5) recovers most of the
trained linear gain, i.e. much of training is attenuation, learned without choosing a
strength on SCORPION val; (3) the nonlinear residual (V1-S vs V0-S, residual-only arm); (4)

V1-S; no ablations), which settles the open pooling question in favor of GAP. Needs, if

Decision 2026-10-18 (user): the paper row stays the augmented V1-S (3 seeds; HEST and LOSO
match it). Augmentation is part of the CGM recipe as built from the start (`docs/experiment7.md`,
section 2), not a selected hyperparameter; the selection is over arms with that recipe
fixed, and it gives V1-S either way. The paper reports the no-aug ablation openly: higher on
SCORPION val/test (7e3c 0.9876 / 0.9780 vs 7b71 0.9852 / 0.9756), −0.002 on PLISM, and claim 7
(augmentation is not the driver of the gain over G0). Do not motivate augmentation by its
PLISM result (that would be tuning on PLISM); motivate it a priori. The augmentations are
geometric only (flips, rot90, zoom = square crop of 80–100% of the area resized back), shared
by the 5 views of a tile, so they vary content and geometry, never stain or the scanner
difference: more distinct content samples for the generative model and a regularizer for
the backbone-in-the-loop training (the likelihood has a train/val gap). No stain
augmentation was used.
Known issue, selection unchanged: `cgm_select` splits ac94 from 7b71/8ed0 because later
configs carry `hold_out_scanner: null` (added with LOSO); pooled mean 0.9853 < the 0.9872
threshold. Only the candidates table is affected. Fixed 2026-10-18 (5df23f1); rerun done
2026-10-18 (`runs/bench/2026-10-18_10-46-17_lxbelshark_0256_scorpion_cgm_select_all`, 0953e0d
with the fix uncommitted, see its `git.txt`): the three V1-S seeds form one candidate (c0),
selection unchanged (no-aug 7e3c). It replaces e198 in the `cgm` block.

  `runs/bench/2026-10-18_15-24-25_lxbelshark_df79_cgm_v1s_noaug_7e3c`, 73af

Per slide pair top-1 (plismbench `metrics.csv`, mean of both directions); Δ = method minus reference, mean over the pairs of the group; 95% CI = percentile interval of a slide-level bootstrap (2000 draws, seed 0; pairs share slides, so slides are resampled and a pair counts once per drawn copy of each of its slides); pairs better = share of pairs with Δ > 0. HR = either slide has the HR stain. One fit per method, so the interval covers the slide sample only, not seeds.

Reference: `cgm_v1s_7b71` (`runs/bench/2026-10-14_23-36-54_lxbelshark_22f4_cgm_v1s_7b71`). Per slide pair top-1 (plismbench `metrics.csv`, mean of both directions); Δ = method minus reference, mean over the pairs of the group; 95% CI = percentile interval of a slide-level bootstrap (2000 draws, seed 0; pairs share slides, so slides are resampled and a pair counts once per drawn copy of each of its slides); pairs better = share of pairs with Δ > 0. HR = either slide has the HR stain. One fit per method, so the interval covers the slide sample only, not seeds.

<!-- /results:plism_pairs -->

**Why HR has its own rows.** HR is one of PLISM's 13 staining conditions (GIV, GIVH, GM,
GMH, GV, GVH, HR, HRH, KR, KRH, LM, LMH, MY) and the one where cross-stain retrieval fails
(roadmap section 1: "known HR-stain failure"). From `runs/bench/2026-10-15_05-38-58_claude-box_6ed5_plism_pairs_gap_g0_cgm_q2_vae/by_stain.csv` (inter-staining
pairs, written by `by_stain.py` in that dir from its `pairs.csv`):
- HR against any other stain: G0 top-1 0.001–0.017 per stain, 0.007 overall, vs 0.357 for
  the inter-staining pairs without HR (per stain 0.209–0.432). Slide-pair cosine 0.690 vs
  0.888 (GAP: 0.611 vs 0.857). HRH, the sibling stain, behaves like the others (0.374
  without HR), so the failure is HR-specific.
- HR against HR on another scanner is only harder: "inter-scanner, HR" above, G0 0.731
  (21 pairs).

Consequences for reading the tables: (1) HR is in 609 of the 4,095 pairs, and its
cross-stain pairs sit near zero for every method, which dilutes the inter-staining /
joint / all means; "all, HR" only shows that a method does not make those pairs worse.
(2) "inter-scanner, HR" is the check for learned maps that break on an unusual stain:
PairedVAE `ysltwjoj` lost there in the CATS phase (archive, PLISM section) and is still
the lowest row; `lazztzda` recovers part of it; the linear maps and CGM stay at G0's
level. Why HR is so far off: checked by eye on the PLISM images (2026-10-15, by the user),
HR is Harris hematoxylin applied overnight (more than 24 h), i.e. a protocol outlier, not a
scanner or method effect; HRH is not overnight and stays in. The without-HR blocks below drop every
pair with HR on either side (`drop_stain=HR`); report them next to the full tables, with
this reason, never instead of them.

Reference: `mixture_G0_K1` (`runs/bench/2026-10-14_07-10-49_lxbelshark_0721_mixture_G0_K1`). Per slide pair top-1 (plismbench `metrics.csv`, mean of both directions); Δ = method minus reference, mean over the pairs of the group; 95% CI = percentile interval of a slide-level bootstrap (2000 draws, seed 0; pairs share slides, so slides are resampled and a pair counts once per drawn copy of each of its slides); pairs better = share of pairs with Δ > 0. HR = either slide has the HR stain. One fit per method, so the interval covers the slide sample only, not seeds. Pairs with the stain(s) HR on either side are dropped before grouping and bootstrapping.

Reference: `h0_mini_gap_ref` (`runs/bench/2026-10-13_11-03-53_lxbelshark_8f0f_h0_mini_gap_ref`). Per gene and fold Pearson from HEST `results_kfold.json` (nothing recomputed), paired on (task, fold, gene); Δ = method minus reference, mean over folds and genes (= the difference of the official task means). 95% CI: per task, a bootstrap over the 50 genes with the folds fixed, i.e. conditional on these test patients, not an interval over patients (2 to 6 folds per task); average row, a bootstrap over the 10 tasks with the genes resampled inside each (2000 draws, seed 0). Folds better = folds whose mean Pearson beats the reference's. One fit per method.

Reference: `mixture_G0_K1` (`runs/bench/2026-10-14_10-08-10_lxbelshark_41cf_mixture_G0_K1`). Per gene and fold Pearson from HEST `results_kfold.json` (nothing recomputed), paired on (task, fold, gene); Δ = method minus reference, mean over folds and genes (= the difference of the official task means). 95% CI: per task, a bootstrap over the 50 genes with the folds fixed, i.e. conditional on these test patients, not an interval over patients (2 to 6 folds per task); average row, a bootstrap over the 10 tasks with the genes resampled inside each (2000 draws, seed 0). Folds better = folds whose mean Pearson beats the reference's. One fit per method.

CGM fits (`map_cgm.pt`) on the shared SCORPION-proxy protocol (`sipe.eval.cgm_select`), clean features. Val: probes trained on train (`summary_val.csv`); selection = the simplest configuration (arm, then larger ρ) whose mean val top-1 gain over G0 (fitted in the run, α = 1) reaches 95% of the best gain (`selected.json`, `candidates.csv`). Test: read once after the selection, probes trained on val (`summary_test.csv`). Config = fits that differ only by seed share an id.

SCORPION val, clean features, probes trained on train (– = `--retrieval-only`; `summary_val.csv`, arms in `arms.json`). G0 = consensus shrinkage (K = 1, α by the 95% rule on val); CGM-init = the V0-S init map (closed form, no training). Data: clean = the GAP cache's train split, other labels = augmented train caches (`sipe.features.extract`; a unit = one tile copy, views share the transform). Units: n = n tiles with one random copy each (same tiles in every arm), tiles = every tile once, all = every copy. Δ vs G0|clean|all (= the G0 of the mixture sweep when α = 1). Explanatory ablation: nothing is selected across arms; the PLISM readout uses the saved `map_<estimator>_<data>_<units>.pt`. Other rows (raw, reference, V0-S, ...) are map files evaluated in the same run. Test (`--test`, read once, `summary_test.csv`, probes trained on val as in `cgm_select`): – = not read. CGM-fp(<noise>) = the stationary point of the CGM training objective with a linear encoder (closed-form V0-S, `sipe.estimators.cgm_fixed_point`).

V0-S objective by block coordinate descent from the CGM-init (`arms.json`, `fp_history_*.csv`, `linear_dist.csv`): k = iterations (conv = to convergence, the optimum: posterior collapse, tr(S_q) = D); J = the training objective per source view and dimension, without constants, at the init and at k; tr(W) / tr(W init) = noise trace over the init's within-tile noise (mean over scanners). Distance = ||L M − L' M'||_F / ||L' M'||_F to each linear map of `--maps`.

Leave-one-scanner-out on SCORPION val or test (`sipe.eval.loso`): each map^-j is fitted on train without scanner j (consensus over the 4 others); unseen = scanner pairs of the split that include j, seen = the other pairs (`summary_<split>.csv`). Δ vs G0^-j = minus G0 fitted the same way (K = 1, α = 1); Δ vs full fit = minus the same estimator fitted on all 5 scanners (the cost of not seeing j). Intervals: slide bootstrap over the 5 WSIs of the split (`contrasts_<split>.csv`); mean rows average the per-j contrasts. An analysis of frozen models: nothing is selected.

Scanner-count study on SCORPION (`sipe.eval.scanner_count`): each map is fitted on the train rows of n scanners of the universe (every subset for the closed-form maps G0, CGM-init, eraser λ = 0.5; the fits given for V0-S / V1-S); full = all train scanners. Scored on the split's pairs of universe scanners: all = every such pair (the same for every subset), seen = both scanners in the subset, unseen = the others. top-1 = mean over the subsets of size n (min–max over subsets); Δ vs G0 = map minus G0 fitted on the same subset, averaged over the subsets, slide-bootstrap interval with the same draws for every subset (`contrasts_<split>.csv`). An analysis of fixed configurations: nothing is selected.

Scanner-count study on PLISM, inter-scanner pairs (same stain, two scanners) split by how many of the pair's two PLISM scanners were in the map's fit subset. PLISM scanners matched to SCORPION by model: AT2 = AT2, GT450 = GT450, P = Philips (same model, not the same instrument); the Hamamatsu scanners (S60, S210, S360, SQ) are never seen, and DP200 / P1000 are not in PLISM. Per pair: mean over the subsets of size n in which the pair falls in the group (a pair can be in several groups at the same n); Δ = map minus G0 of the same subset; 95% CI = slide-level bootstrap (2000 draws, seed 0); pairs = number of PLISM slide pairs in the group. Same runs as the scanner_count_plism block.

### Second backbone: UNI2-h (CLS token), raw / G0 / V1-S

Same pipeline as H0-mini (`scripts/uni2h_run.sh`, run dirs in `runs/uni2h_manifest.env`),
nothing re-selected: V1-S with the H0-mini hyperparameters
(`configs/consensus_gen_uni2h.yaml`), one seed. UNI2-h is pooled on its CLS token, H0-mini
on patch-token GAP, so compare each backbone's rows with its own raw row only.

CGM fits (`map_cgm.pt`) on the shared SCORPION-proxy protocol (`sipe.eval.cgm_select`), clean features. Val: probes trained on train (`summary_val.csv`); selection = the simplest configuration (arm, then larger ρ) whose mean val top-1 gain over G0 (fitted in the run, α = 1) reaches 95% of the best gain (`selected.json`, `candidates.csv`). Test: read once after the selection, probes trained on val (`summary_test.csv`). Config = fits that differ only by seed share an id.

Per slide pair top-1 (plismbench `metrics.csv`, mean of both directions); Δ = method minus reference, mean over the pairs of the group; 95% CI = percentile interval of a slide-level bootstrap (2000 draws, seed 0; pairs share slides, so slides are resampled and a pair counts once per drawn copy of each of its slides); pairs better = share of pairs with Δ > 0. HR = either slide has the HR stain. One fit per method, so the interval covers the slide sample only, not seeds. Pairs with the stain(s) HR on either side are dropped before grouping and bootstrapping.

### Pooling sensitivity: UNI2-h on patch-token GAP, raw / G0 / V1-S

Same pipeline, `POOLING=gap scripts/backbone_run.sh` at bb132c4 (clean), run dirs in
`runs/uni2h_gap_manifest.env`; the cache is the CLS run's
(`runs/bench/2026-10-20_21-55-38_lxbelshark_7578_uni2h_cache`, it holds both poolings).
V1-S with the H0-mini hyperparameters, one seed, nothing re-selected. A pooling-sensitivity
check, not a selection: the paper's UNI2-h rows stay on CLS (its official embedding).
Compare each pooling's rows with its own raw row.

### Third backbone: CONCH v1.5 (official attentional-pooler head), raw / G0 / V1-S

Same pipeline, `ENCODER=conch-v1.5 scripts/backbone_run.sh`, run dirs in
`runs/conch15_manifest.env`; loader matched to TRIDENT (`docs/findings.md` 16), so the
plismbench leaderboard row of CONCH v1.5 is not directly comparable. Embedding = pooling
`head` (768-d), fixed beforehand. V1-S with the H0-mini hyperparameters, one seed, nothing
re-selected.

Test: read once after the selection, probes trained on val (`summary_test.csv`). Config = fits that differ only by seed share an id.

Per gene and fold Pearson from HEST `results_kfold.json` (nothing recomputed), paired on (task, fold, gene); Δ = method minus reference, mean over folds and genes (= the difference of the official task means). 95% CI: per task, a bootstrap over the 50 genes with the folds fixed, i.e. conditional on these test patients, not an interval over patients (2 to 6 folds per task); average row, a bootstrap over the 10 tasks with the genes resampled inside each (2000 draws, seed 0). Folds better = folds whose mean Pearson beats the reference's. One fit per method.

### Pooling sensitivity: CONCH v1.5 on patch-token GAP, raw / G0 / V1-S

Same pipeline, `POOLING=gap ENCODER=conch-v1.5 scripts/backbone_run.sh` (fit and G0 at 98d5bdf,
the rest at 816db3e, a docs-only change; all clean), run dirs in `runs/conch15_gap_manifest.env`; the cache is the head run's
(`runs/bench/2026-10-22_10-28-12_lxbelshark_6f99_conch15_cache`, it holds `head`, `cls` and
`gap`; GAP = 1024-d trunk). V1-S with the H0-mini hyperparameters, one seed, nothing
re-selected. A pooling-sensitivity check, not a selection: the paper's CONCH rows stay on the
official head. Compare each pooling's rows with its own raw row.
